# Consolidated changes

The manuscript images are the primary evidence. C and H are independently fallible AI witnesses; S is a prior sweep decision. A row denotes an editorially distinct place, not a token. Counts include resolved witness doubts and changes of deletion/insertion state where recorded. Macro-only differences and newly added uncertainty alone are excluded. The same place may count against both witnesses.

## Consequential corrections

- Scan 43, opening, I inclusion: The opening writes I subset S double-prime, not H’s single prime. The apparent set-theoretic slip is retained; intended correction is discussed only in notes.
- Scan 44: The repeated relation is non panachés, not H’s non parallèles (044-C12, C24, C26).
- Scan 44: The four-point example has cardinal 2, not H’s 1, and carré (044-C17, C21).
- Scan 44: The added generalization reads A ou B, not H’s A cap B (044-C16).
- Scan 44: The geometric note lacks H’s du bord: the written hypothesis is partie d’un disque convexe (044-C27–28); no boundary condition is supplied.
- Scan 45, opening adjective after mutuellement non: The defining adjective is panachées, not parallèles. This changes the mathematical terminology of the class of partitions.
- Scan 45, margin, remaining example before S: The marginal coarse partition has just one class equal to S, contrary to H’s au moins deux.
- Scan 46: Scan046, second condition: preserve the literal endpoint-exclusion wording rather than H’s interior paraphrase.
- Scan 46: Scan046, marginal union proof: preserve the clear written x in Sigma; x in A is the required mathematical condition, recorded only in notes.
- Scan 46: Scan046, discarded proof: retain the independently struck donc and the unfinished final equality.
- Scan 47: The lower restriction in the R-star formula is A proper subset R, not A not equal R (047-05).
- Scan 47: The slanted marginal word is faux, beside the reconstruction claim (047-28).
- Scan 48: The separation formula reads on a B-star cap A = phi, ou B-star subset A; H’s ou after the quantifier is not supported.
- Scan 48: The condition range is written with baseline numerals and a colon; the script relation capital, straight dotted R and inverted-Pi coproduct remain distinct.
- Scan 48: The example and its cancelled successor retain the observed drawing labels and ambiguous S-plus-tick forms; neither is repaired from the mathematical expectation.
- Scan 49: The closure clause explicitly restricts unions and intersections to those that give segments; H omits this condition and reverses the operation order.
- Scan 49: The proof states the nonempty qualification in its minimality parenthesis; the equality sign after et is retained literally.
- Scan 49: The lower contradiction uses the cocomponent contained in A and the formula A_i cap A_j = phi; omitted and altered H wording is restored from the crop.
- Scan 50: The family insertion reads l’ens des onglets, not C’s l’un; the family and union indices remain uncertain.
- Scan 50: The successive live corrections to R1 are preserved, and the residual-set letter is distinguished from the script relation letter where the ink supports it.
- Scan 50: The full cocomponent argument and its membership sign are restored against H’s omissions and altered subset wording.

### Still unresolved

- b), overwritten letter before a-prime: `b'`; alternatives: []; medium. The tall b-like stem is present but the lower bowl is obscured by the final a; retain doubt within deletion. Crop 044-01.
- b), cancelled les ordres: `les ordres`; alternatives: ["les ord."]; low. The tall d is visible; the cancellation merges with the last letters and possible stop. Crop 044-02.
- Quand paragraph, capitalisation: `Parmi`; alternatives: ["parmi"]; medium. Large upper P bowl and long stem favour the capital; the hand can enlarge initials. Crop 044-08. Both cases are marked in place.
- d), parenthesis before l’enveloppe: `au choix`; alternatives: ["au besoin"]; medium. Reduced au and a short following group with possible ch; fading and overlaps prevent exclusion of the longer alternative. Crops 044-16 and 044-19.
- Example, struck words after Si: `$A$ et`; alternatives: []; low. A-like diagonals and the tall second form survive, but not every letter is plain. Crop 044-10.
- Last main formula, family alphabet: `\mathfrak P_2`; alternatives: ["\\mathcal P_2", "P_2"]; medium. Broad cap and angular bowl favour Gothic P. Neither body instance securely fixes the alphabet; the full formula was read. Crop 044-15.
- e), opening cancelled words: `On a`; alternatives: []; low. O/n and a-like lower strokes cross the cancellation. Crops 044-17 and 044-20.
- e), after the first C: `$C=$`; alternatives: ["un"]; low. A curved letter-like remnant and two short bars are crossed by the long strike. Crops 044-17 and 044-20.
- e), second line beginning: `on a des`; alternatives: []; low. A low o/n outline and final s are partly visible; the middle letters coincide with the strike. Crops 044-17 and 044-20.
- e), after barred B: `ds`; alternatives: ["de"]; low. Short downstroke and trailing curl are inseparable from the cancellation. Crops 044-17 and 044-20.
- e), short form before the final formula: `st`; alternatives: []; low. A tall t-like stroke and preceding low mark survive; they do not justify an expansion. Crops 044-17 and 044-20.
- e), final cancelled group: `$= A$`; alternatives: []; low. Two faint horizontals and A remain but the relation to the preceding cancelled formula is not fully readable. Crops 044-17 and 044-20.
- Cor, after De plus: `lorsque ces`; alternatives: []; low. Reduced loops before conditions are entangled with the horizontal body text. Crops 044-18 and 044-21.
- Cor, after conditions: `sont`; alternatives: []; low. Tiny upper mark and low connecting strokes are present; the clause is not grammatically completed from expectation. Crops 044-18 and 044-21.
- Cor, opening of parenthesis: `donc`; alternatives: ["alors"]; medium. The compressed initial and ending before A et B remain hard to separate. Crops 044-18 and 044-22.
- Cor, final components phrase: `composants de $A$ et $B$`; alternatives: ["composantes de $A$ et $B$"]; medium. Compos- and t are visible but the possible e before s is at a crossing. Crops 044-18 and 044-23.
- b), final word of the last equivalent clause: `opposés`; alternatives: voisins; medium. The final short baseline has two deep adjacent strokes supporting doubled p and a short terminal accented-looking group, favouring opposés; the crossing margin obscures its beginning and leaves the blind voisins reading possible. Retain opposés as preferred but add voisins as an alternative. No second pas is visible. Root additionally inspected the two new word crops; the wider one prevents clipping of the initial overlap.
- Cor, raised underlined word above existe: `donc`; alternatives: deux; medium. The opening has De plus, including a p descender, followed by the already qualified crossing words. Dans le cas où, the square-bottomed union, and the final separate subset C line remain visible. The raised word above existe has a d-like beginning and compressed terminal strokes compatible with donc or deux; it has a separate short underline in addition to the long attachment curve. Add the alternative and the underline; do not replace the surrounding passage with the blind speculative wording.

### Still unresolved

- opening parenthetical adjective: `panachées`; alternatives: ["panachés"]; medium. c04: body panach- and accent are secure; the pen joins the last e/s strokes too closely to count the e forms independently.
- margin after grossière,: `ayant`; alternatives: []; medium. c06: initial oval and final long turn fit ayant; compressed middle letters prevent a fully secure reading.
- margin after S: `.\,--`; alternatives: ["\\ldots"]; medium. c06: point plus extended low trail is visible; whether the trail represents a dash or elongated remaining dots is uncertain.
- cancelled phrase after À: `une tel`; alternatives: ["un tel"]; low. c07: cancellation lies on the first word's low bodies; a terminal e cannot be certified or ruled out. The grammatical mismatch is not corrected.
- overwritten group in cardinal insertion: `Card.`; alternatives: []; low. c09: apparent previous Card. is largely superposed; neither every letter nor its complete extent is independently recoverable. Retained inside deleted/unsure, not silently resolved.
- live initial in cardinal insertion: `Cardinal`; alternatives: ["cardinal"]; low. c09: the surviving open initial is relatively large but lies among overwritten strokes.
- Second cardinality parenthesis, replacement Cardinal/cardinal after cancelled cardin/cardinal: `Cardinal`; alternatives: ["cardinal"]; medium. c12: large initial open C form; local size contrast is not sufficient to certify upper rather than lower case.
- Second cardinality parenthesis, cancelled word: `cardin`; alternatives: ["cardinal"]; medium. The blind crop shows a separate de before the cancellation; the cancelled group begins with an open c, then low a/r, a tall d stem, a dotted i and a low n-like ending. It does not show ordre. The last strokes are covered by the strike, so cardin is preferred with cardinal retained as an alternative. This supersedes the first-pass ordre reading. ### Still unresolved

- First condition, overwritten sign: `\Sigma`; alternatives: [">"]; low. Angular old strokes are crossed by the live looped sigma; first-label crop does not reveal the entire old form.
- Definition, tiny mark after equals: `.`; alternatives: ["\\cdot", "non-text speck / omission"]; low. A small dark low mark is present. It is not a centered operator, and its function as punctuation cannot yet be distinguished from an incidental spot.
- Mapping target alphabet: `P`; alternatives: ["\\mathcal P", "\\mathfrak P"]; low. Single long stem with square bowl and broad low return; font identity remains ambiguous. Draft now records all three candidate alphabets. This is an uncertainty-candidate extension, not a C reading correction.
- Marginal cancelled phrase: `sont disj.`; alternatives: ["sont di"]; low. Parallel strike lines obscure the middle letters; not every letter is legible.
- Cancelled argument, incomplete A expression: `A^*\supset`; alternatives: ["A\\supset"]; low. A small cluster at upper right of A is entangled with the following relation and cancellation; retain the star doubt.
- Cancelled argument, final prose: `on aurait`; alternatives: ["on a"]; low. The box bottom crosses the following connected word; the middle letters cannot all be separated.
- Marginal least-member wording, terminal form: `élément`; alternatives: ["élém\\textsuperscript{t}"]; medium. The rotated fresh crop confirms both indexed unions, the raised Sigma(x), the clear literal x in Sigma, and tot. with its stop. At the end of élément the compressed terminal strokes do not securely distinguish the full form from élém with raised t; that uncertainty is now explicit. The earlier punctuation readings are retained.
- Intersecting case, reduced marks before citation: `on a`; alternatives: ["$\\cdots$"]; low. Before the raised par sigma 1, the three reduced marks can be read as highly compressed on a or as an ellipsis. The image does not settle their status, so both are retained in place. The leading Si is visible at the right of b10.
- Live conclusion, abbreviated closing formula: `qfd.`; alternatives: ["cqfd."]; medium. The formula and cancelled Je dis are intact. The closing abbreviation has a descending q, tall following strokes and a stop; a separate initial c is not secure, so qfd./cqfd. is retained as an explicit alternative. The diagonal subtraction stroke in S setminus B-star is retained.

- cancelled word under i.e.: `donc`; alternatives: []; low. c12: d-like upright and final c survive, middle letters concealed by strike.
- between R est and la fois: `:`; alternatives: ["à"]; medium. c12: two near-vertical marks, with no secure lower a loop; à fits the phrase but is not silently substituted.
- parenthetical NB first phrase: `c'est`; alternatives: []; medium. The close fresh crop shows an older descending p-like stroke and a low point beneath the higher c/apostrophe/est group. The underlayer was omitted in the first pass. Restore deleted p.ex/p. as uncertain and mark the superposed c’est as an insertion, retaining its lexical qualification. The final p. ex still has no added terminal stop.
- cancelled words after reconstruction que: `on a`; alternatives: ["est"]; low. c17: horizontal strokes obscure initial and ending; both C alternatives remain possible.
- addition above défini: `connu`; alternatives: ["connus"]; medium. c18: low connected final return does not distinguish u from us securely.
- NB, terminal form before raised A: `élément`; alternatives: ["élém"]; medium. The word before the raised A has clear initial élé- and a long low terminal run, but the final n/t cannot be individually separated. Add élém as an abbreviated alternative to élément. The independently cancelled on remains qualified because its middle is crossed.
- Parenthetical NB, overwritten underlayer: `p.ex`; alternatives: ["p."]; low. The close fresh crop shows an older descending p-like stroke and a low point beneath the higher c/apostrophe/est group. The underlayer was omitted in the first pass. Restore deleted p.ex/p. as uncertain and mark the superposed c’est as an insertion, retaining its lexical qualification. The final p. ex still has no added terminal stop.

### Still unresolved

- Onglets initial vowel: `onglets`; alternatives: ["anglets"]; medium. c01: both occurrences have compressed initial vowels; qualified at first occurrence only.
- Opening struck word: `dis`; alternatives: ["des", "de"]; low. After l-apostrophe, the opening noun has a small e turn, connected n and terminal s hook; resolve l'ens, without supplying a stop. The raised relation capital has a large closed left oval and separate upper return/leg, resolving script R1. The onglet vowels remain compressed and qualified at their first occurrence. The cancelled d-group lacks a secure i dot, so dis gains des/de alternatives. In the raised phrase the low un and separate de end without a separate s; retain Aussi l'un de and the underlying ces.
- Curve before segments: `(`; alternatives: []; low. c02: separated upright curve may be a parenthesis; no balancing mark is supplied.
- Short cancellation before distinctes: `di`; alternatives: ["dis"]; low. c04: loop and short crossed strokes do not settle the ending.
- sont before mutuellement: `sont`; alternatives: []; medium. c05: first stroke and heavy baseline leave the compressed word partly obscured.
- Dot before de réunion: `\cdot`; alternatives: ["."]; low. c08: isolated round dark point can be a line marker or stop.
- NB abbreviation: `segm.`; alternatives: ["segmts", "segm"]; low. c08: g descender and compressed termination do not decide a point or small letters.
- Upper marginal cancelled sign: `\geq`; alternatives: ["\\to"]; low. c11: sign merges with cancellation; no separate 1 is secure.
- Final recurrence subscript: `i-1`; alternatives: ["i"]; low. c09: dotted i is clear; short dash and terminal upright are faint/overlapped.
- Cancelled marginal word: `donc`; alternatives: []; low. c11: rising stroke crosses its low interior.
- Underlying A before B: `A`; alternatives: []; low. c10: diagonal underlayer behind B is not fully separable.
- Last cancelled word: `les`; alternatives: ["tout", "tel"]; low. c10: tall initial and low joined strokes are crossed.
- Cancelled de: `de`; alternatives: []; low. c13: small crossed group resembles de, but not every letter is separable.
- Cancelled boundary expression: `\partial B`; alternatives: ["B"]; low. c13: B is clear but the preceding curved sign may belong to cancellation.
- Left-column mut abbreviation: `mut\textsuperscript{t}`; alternatives: ["mut\\textsuperscript{lt}"]; medium. c14: raised terminal stroke(s) remain compressed.
- Cancelled phrase beneath cardinality insertion: `de cardinal`; alternatives: []; low. c14: some letters are covered by the strike and the raised formula.
- Cancelled cardinal bound digit: `3`; alternatives: ["2"]; medium. c16: two lobes favor 3, but overlaid crossing strokes leave 2 as a residual alternative.
- Underlying symbol before S_2: `C`; alternatives: ["\\Sigma", "S"]; low. Beneath the heavy live S there is a broad rounded upper arc and a rounded lower return, favoring an older C rather than an angular Sigma. The overwritten path remains incomplete, so retain Sigma and S alternatives. The following live2, set difference and T2 are clear. The component symbol has a flat cap and two straight legs, leaving pi/Pi case unresolved; the lower oval0 and crossed not-equal1 are clear.
- Final pi alphabet/case: `\pi_0`; alternatives: ["\\Pi_0"]; medium. c20: straight top and two upright legs permit either case; the separate low oval 0 is secure.
- Raised R-qualified adjective: `saturés`; alternatives: ["admis"]; medium. The raised word has a small angular initial, a tall medial stroke, compressed following turns, and a short mark over the final group. These favor saturés but do not fully exclude admis in the tiny writing, so add that alternative. The insertion has its own curved leader and parentheses. The baseline cardinal operator begins with the same low curved c as the local prose short form, retaining lowercase card.

### Still unresolved

- P2 family alphabet in the first definition and both occurrences in condition b (052-03, 052-12): `\mathcal{P}_2`; alternatives: ["\\mathfrak{P}_2", "P_2"]; medium. The tall stem and broad angular bowl with a lower return are clear, as is the subscript 2. Those strokes do not securely select ordinary, calligraphic or Gothic P; all three local alphabet possibilities remain qualified.
- Cancelled noun before inserted segments (052-15): `intervalles`; alternatives: []; low. The beginning and tall later strokes favour intervalles; the cancellation hides middle letters, so the struck word remains under a doubt mark.
- Cancelled group after compos. (052-16): `de`; alternatives: ["des"]; low. The d loop survives, but the horizontal strike hides whether the low ending is e alone or e followed by s.
- Accent in extrêmes (052-17): `extrêmes`; alternatives: ["extremes"]; medium. No clean detached circumflex can be isolated above the relevant low letter in this tightly written group. The original accented and unaccented candidates are retained without an uncertainty-only change row.
- Cancelled capital under B in condition b (052-23): `A`; alternatives: ["B"]; low. Lighter diagonal strokes survive beneath the dark rounded B, but not enough of the earlier letter is open to settle A against B.
- Overwritten operation between B and boundary A in condition b (052-23): `\cap`; alternatives: []; medium. Under the first live B, lighter diagonal strokes remain A/B. No complete older B can be separated under the final A of the inclusion. At the operation before boundary A, two short parallel horizontal strokes are visible beneath an overtraced arch: record a qualified earlier equality, with A as alternative, before the still-qualified cap. The modified inclusion and long-stem phi are unchanged.
- Cancelled quantifier at start of condition c (052-24): `\forall`; alternatives: ["\\forall\\varepsilon"]; low. The crossed uprights form a forall-like outline. Crowded strokes at its right do not establish whether epsilon was also written.
- Live words after the cancellation in condition c (052-24): `On a`; alternatives: ["on a"]; medium. The low connected words are present before boundary I, but their first loop overlaps the cancellation and does not fix the capitalisation.
- Cancelled short group before deux (052-26): `s\textsuperscript{t}`; alternatives: ["à", "$A$"]; low. The tiny cancelled group before deux has a low entry and a small peaked upper body, compatible with the existing abbreviated s-raised-t or a-grave candidates and also a capital A. Add A as a candidate without expanding a verb. The final cancelled word ends de, with no following u-x.
- Cancelled word between posons and alors (052-28): `$\Sigma$`; alternatives: ["$\\hat{\\Sigma}$", "en"]; low. The cancelled group after posons has a compact zigzag and lower bar, favoring Sigma; the upper overwritten strokes leave a hatted Sigma possible, while en remains a low-confidence alternative. The following word has a tall loop and compressed low returns; retain alors as preferred but admit les/l'ens. The separate cancelled R equals is already recorded in the page.
- Slanted note, compressed word before l’autre: `pas`; alternatives: []; medium. The opening contains the low ou group and longer e-n-c-o-r-e sequence, with no initial accented a or m of a moins. A,B is retained. At the end, the p-like long stroke of pas is visible but its following flattened run is crossed by adjacent writing and does not separately expose a and s; retain pas with a doubt mark.
- Family condition a, possible comma attached to A: `,`; alternatives: []; medium. The initial S reaches the capital height, supporting Si. The descending curved stroke beside the first A could be its extended return or an attached comma, so qualify that first comma. After the later A subset B, the small stroke belongs to the o of ou and is not another comma. The written on a and all three alternatives remain.
- Earlier operation beneath the live cap before boundary A: `=`; alternatives: ["A"]; low. Under the first live B, lighter diagonal strokes remain A/B. No complete older B can be separated under the final A of the inclusion. At the operation before boundary A, two short parallel horizontal strokes are visible beneath an overtraced arch: record a qualified earlier equality, with A as alternative, before the still-qualified cap. The modified inclusion and long-stem phi are unchanged.
- Live connective after the cancelled group following posons: `alors`; alternatives: ["les", "l'ens"]; low. The cancelled group after posons has a compact zigzag and lower bar, favoring Sigma; the upper overwritten strokes leave a hatted Sigma possible, while en remains a low-confidence alternative. The following word has a tall loop and compressed low returns; retain alors as preferred but admit les/l'ens. The separate cancelled R equals is already recorded in the page.

The compact dark chord mark has a left hooked branch and heavier right descent, so gamma/4 remains qualified rather than resolved as1. ### Decisions excluded from correction counts

Retained alternatives, new uncertainty alone, candidate ordering, and editorial witness descriptions do not add a correction count.

| ID | Place | C | H | Final | Image decision | Confidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 054-U01 | Right g equation operator | g=g'\unsure{-&#124;+}g'' | g=g'+g'' | g=g'\unsure{+&#124;-}g'' | The crop shows a short descending component within the horizontal stroke, curling into g-double-prime. This supports plus as preferred, but continuity with the following letter prevents an unqualified decision. Root personally inspected the crop and adopted this qualified reading. S SA01-K81-03 is supported as a preferred candidate, while its claimed certainty is overturned.; `crops/scan054/right_formula_count.png` (975, 780, 1645, 985) | medium |
| 054-U02 | Underlined inserted technical word, additional alternative | \unsure{birives&#124;monorives&#124;bivives} | binaires | \unsure{birives&#124;monorives&#124;bivives&#124;binoires} | The raised technical word retains a crowded opening and compressed middle. Its low hump and small oval also permit binoires as an additional local candidate; keep birives preferred and retain the earlier alternatives. The first variable is still a/e. The lower opposées is cancelled and replaced by the raised group with its curved leader. The baseline has tels: one tall l and no second l/e run that would support telles.; `crops/second054_R2/054-b04.png` (60, 470, 735, 625) | low |

### Still unresolved

- First heading underlying overwritten word: `\unsure{birives|bifidées}`; alternatives: ["birives", "bifidées"]; low. The middle is hidden by the replacement; not all underlying letters are legible.
- First heading replacement: `\unsure{bifides}`; alternatives: ["bifides"]; medium. Crossed f, dotted i and tall d are visible; the first and last letters are entangled with earlier ink.
- First inserted letter: `\unsure{a|e}`; alternatives: ["a", "e"]; medium. The small loop opens at upper left; the following a-prime and ordered-pair letters favour a but do not fully settle this local form.
- Underlined left insertion: `\unsure{birives|monorives|bivives|binoires}`; alternatives: ["birives", "monorives", "bivives", "binoires"]; low. The raised technical word retains a crowded opening and compressed middle. Its low hump and small oval also permit binoires as an additional local candidate; keep birives preferred and retain the earlier alternatives. The first variable is still a/e. The lower opposées is cancelled and replaced by the raised group with its curved leader. The baseline has tels: one tall l and no second l/e run that would support telles.
- Comma between first two ordered pairs: `\unsure{,}`; alternatives: [","]; medium. A dark descending mark lies between the first closing and second opening parenthesis but joins the latter’s stroke.
- Cancelled word inside raised d’arcs addition: `\unsure{paire|paires}`; alternatives: ["paire", "paires"]; low. The baseline cancellation conceals the middle and terminal extent; both short and plural forms remain possible.
- Cancelled mais: `\unsure{mais}`; alternatives: ["mais"]; low. The horizontal cancellation removes much of the interior; only the opening and terminal returns and an i dot remain.
- Left f equation operator: `\unsure{+|-}`; alternatives: ["+", "-"]; medium. The short horizontal mark has a small raised/turned portion joining the next f. Plus is preferred, but a separate full vertical cross is not securely isolated.
- Right heading monorive: `\unsure{monorive|monovive}`; alternatives: ["monorive", "monovive"]; medium. The narrow stroke after mono and before dotted i can be r or v; the clearer lower plural supports r without deleting the local alternative.
- Right paire/pair: `\unsure{paire|pair}`; alternatives: ["paire", "pair"]; medium. The terminal after r is short and may be e or a pen return; the noun remains unexpanded and qualified.
- Right opening parenthesis: `\unsure{(}`; alternatives: ["("]; low. A narrow upright stands just to the left of the struck b and could be the matching parenthesis; its separation from the overwritten letter is not complete.
- Right cancelled term: `\unsure{birives}`; alternatives: ["birives"]; low. Two i-dot positions and initial b are visible, but the strike conceals intervening letters.
- Right g equation operator: `\unsure{+|-}`; alternatives: ["+", "-"]; medium. A short downward component branches within the horizontal stroke and curls into g-double-prime, making plus the preferred candidate. Because that component and the next letter’s entry are continuous, minus remains possible. This reorders C’s candidates only and adds qualification to H/S, without a C/H correction count.
- Central underlying overwritten term: `\unsure{bifides|bifidées}`; alternatives: ["bifides", "bifidées"]; low. The b-like opening and upright middle survive, but the live replacement hides the full ending.
- Central replacement: `\unsure{monorives}`; alternatives: ["monorives"]; medium. The final mono… and rives-like forms lie over the earlier term, leaving the middle uncertain.
- Small mark over circle chord: `\unsure{$\gamma$|$4$}`; alternatives: ["\\gamma", "4"]; low. The compact hooked/branched ink allows gamma or 4. Its status as a figure label or aborted mark is uncertain; it remains explicit text rather than an uninterpreted Bézier.

### Still unresolved

- Isolated mark near x534,y187: `Excluded as probable transferred/show-through ink`; alternatives: ["Intentional short graphic mark on this face"]; medium. c05: a blot and pale offsets, with no continuous pencil path or label. Root personally compared the opposite-face mon/bi drawing and identified the corresponding reflected upper arrow; root approves omission at medium confidence. R3 did not view the other page.
- Second partial long edge in the lower sheet: `A separate faint partial pencil run below the main edge`; alternatives: ["A smudged retracing of the main edge rather than a separate contour"]; medium. c03: two close runs remain visibly offset through the middle and right part of the long edge. Both are drawn without assigning them distinct topological meanings.
- Short deposit before the heavy front rim: `Short isolated stroke with a gap before the rim`; alternatives: ["A faint continuation joining the deposit to the rim"]; medium. c01: the dark deposit is separated from the heavier curved stroke by visible pale paper; no continuous dark bridge can be followed.
